#2535. EA SPORTS is looking for football experts for Liga NOS to join our community!

Are you the most knowledgeable football fan around? Do you want to be involved with EA SPORTS FIFA? Well, you’re in luck as the FIFA Talent Scout team is looking for people like you.

The FIFA Talent Scout community has over 6000 active members and is always looking for individuals to help improve their football database.

This is a great opportunity to put your knowledge of your club to good use, helping to ensure that the EA SPORTS football database continues to contain the most up to date and accurate team and player data for the many leagues featured in FIFA 17.

The first step into our community is as a football analyst, who can examine team and player data and offer feedback suggesting possible changes. You can contribute towards improving player appearance, player attributes, team formations and team tactics. There is no minimum or maximum expectation in terms of your contribution to the database. The key is that when you do contribute, your feedback helps to improve the authenticity of FIFA 17.

Those who stand out may be offered the chance to join our editing team and the chance to take full responsibility for a team and its squad. We are able to offer some financial compensation to editors, but in return we do require that you are a very active contributor who works to our deadlines and in line with our guidelines.

#2525. Portuguese football around the web: from Port Vale to Seixal

In a new feature PortuGOAL will be bringing you some recommended reading about Portuguese football from around the web, penned by our own network of writers or others.

We kick off by alerting you to a superb interview in the Guardian by Nick Ames with Port Vale's Portuguese manager Bruno Ribeiro. Benfica are top of the Liga NOS and their coach Rui Vitória continues to confound the doubters who believed he would not be up to the job at the Lisbon giants. Simon Curtis takes a look at "the quiet man at the helm of a national institution". And Benfica fans, while we've got your attention, a shout out for Tom Kundert's profile of the club's world-renowned Seixal Academy for ESPN.

Talking about Portuguese football schools, none has produced more top-class players in recent decades than Sporting's Alcochete Academy, and the excellent Outside of the Boot website has a brilliantly detailed profile written by Mateus Carvalho of another possible gem for the Lions, Francisco Geraldes, who is currently on loan at Moreirense.

To finish up this initial trip around the net, we alert you to FourFourTwo's list of the 59 best Under-21 footballers from around the world. #2523. England’s Dutch coach Sarina Wiegman extends contract until 2029

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

Dutch football coach Sarina Wiegman has extended her contract with the England women’s team by two years, taking her up to the 2029 European Championships.

Wiegman, 56, led the team to two European titles in 2022 and 2025, as well as their first World Cup final in 2023, when they lost to Spain.

Prior to that she won the European Championships with the Dutch national team in 2017 and reached the World Cup final two years later, losing 2-0 to the United States.

“I am so happy to be able to carry on with this squad and cannot think of a better job in football,” she said in a statement released by the English Football Association (FA).

“Working for a world-class organisation like the FA, and alongside so many people dedicated to winning England teams and the growth of our game, excites me every day.”

FA chief executive Mark Bullingham described Wiegman as “an incredible coach with an unparalleled record in major tournaments”. He added: “There is no one better to lead the Lionesses into the next two tournaments.”

Commentators had widely expected the Dutchwoman to leave her post after next year’s World Cup, after England failed to qualify automatically for the tournament.

A 4-0 loss to Spain in June, the team’s heaviest defeat since Wiegman took charge, means they will have to come through a play-off tournament with Greece, Ukraine and Slovakia to reach the finals in Brazil.

Wiegman said: “Anyone who knows me will understand that my main concern right now is absolutely the World Cup, and helping this squad realise their potential in the years to come.

“We have an important job to do to confirm our place in Brazil, and hopefully we can do that and give the country another special summer to remember next year.”

#2522. "It's going to be amazing" – Campbell on Dortmund return

Signal Iduna Park was home for Cole Campbell for four years, but on Saturday, the American will be on the opposite side. The forward left Borussia Dortmund for newly-promoted Elversberg in the summer, where he has hit the ground running.

"I’ve had confidence in what I can do, and I am very grateful that Elversberg has put the trust in me and given me the confidence back," said Campbell in an interview with the DFL. "It’s been amazing to be here and to feel the trust and confidence that they have in me, so I am very happy to be here."

Campbell scored his first Bundesliga goal in Elversberg's opener, a surprise 3-2 win over Bayer Leverkusen. Since then, he's been moved to more of a bench role, partially due to injuries and partially to the emergence of other Elversberg attackers, like Maurice Krattenmacher.

Still, Campbell has made an impact when he's played. He scored with an acrobatic kick against Mainz to clinch a 4-1 win, taking Elversberg up to seventh in the Bundesliga. It was just his second top-flight goal.

“It was an amazing feeling for me to score a goal like this,” said Campbell. "I was very happy after the game and was also happy to have helped the team secure the win."

Campbell was only 18 when he made his debut in the Champions League, coming off the bench in front of a packed stadium against Sturm Graz. He replaced Jamie Gittens as Dortmund, then led by Nuri Şahin, snatched a dramatic 1-0 win at the death.

“At that point, it was probably one of my best feelings, and it was a dream come true to make my debut in the Champions League,” said Campbell. "To play in the Signal Iduna Park was also a different feeling, and I am really looking forward to getting to play there again this weekend. It was an amazing feeling for me.”

After four years and just seven appearances, Campbell left Dortmund in search of minutes. He joined Hoffenheim on loan last January, but injuries limited him to only five appearances. He was an unused substitute when Hoffenheim took on Dortmund in April.

That is unlikely to be the case this season. With the exception of the Augsburg match, which he missed because of an injury, Campbell has played every match this season for Elversberg. Even if he doesn't start, it's still likely he comes off the bench for a reunion with Niko Kovač's Borussia Dortmund and Signal Iduna Park.

“It’s a lot of emotions, and it will be very weird to be there and to not be in a Dortmund jersey,” said Campbell. "I am really looking forward to it, and I am very excited to see my friends and old teammates. I think it will be a special moment for me.”

Campbell debuted for the United States national team in October, coming off the bench in a 1-0 win over Canada.

The winger was also eligible for Iceland through his mother's side, initially playing there at youth level. While he's committed to the United States, Campbell takes the influence of both countries with him.

“It’s a mix of both,” said Campbell in an interview with the DFL. "I would say I take a little bit of both, but I feel more at home in the US for sure.”

Campbell was born in Houston, Texas, but his mother was part of the Iceland national team. He moved to Iceland at a young age, and when he was 15, Campbell was playing in Iceland's top flight. He stayed in the country for two years before leaving for Borussia Dortmund.

“I would say I feel more American, but I also like certain parts of Iceland, and a lot of my friends are from there,” said Campbell. “My mom, my grandma, and lots of my family are from there, so I love to go back there to visit."

#2521. Pinamonti segna e pesa: il 52% dei suoi gol è stato decisivo

Il gol al Cagliari aggiorna il bilancio: dal 2021/22, 26 delle sue 50 reti in Serie A hanno inciso sul risultato.

Non conta soltanto quanto segna un attaccante, ma anche quanto incidono le sue reti. Andrea Pinamonti offre una fotografia significativa sotto questo aspetto: dalla stagione 2021/22, il 52% dei suoi gol in Serie A è risultato decisivo.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il centravanti ha realizzato 26 reti decisive su 50 complessive, contribuendo a un saldo di 38 punti. Nel conteggio rientra anche il gol del definitivo 2-1 della Lazio contro il Cagliari.

Il periodo analizzato parte dal 2021/22, stagione nella quale l’attaccante classe 1999 ha raggiunto per la prima volta la doppia cifra di reti in Serie A.

Da allora, più della metà dei suoi gol ha avuto un peso sul risultato. Il dato dei 38 punti va letto secondo il criterio indicato nella rilevazione: rappresenta la differenza rispetto ai punti che le sue squadre avrebbero ottenuto sottraendo le reti considerate decisive.

Una statistica che mette in evidenza l’incisività del centravanti e aggiunge una chiave di lettura al suo rendimento realizzativo.

Il contributo più consistente arriva dall’esperienza al Sassuolo: 25 gol complessivi, dei quali 13 decisivi, per un totale di 20 punti attribuiti alle sue marcature.

Con l’Empoli, Pinamonti ha invece firmato 13 reti: 7 decisive, con un’incidenza di 10 punti. Al Genoa, il bilancio riportato è di 10 gol, dei quali 5 decisivi, per 6 punti.

Tre esperienze che, insieme ai primi numeri in biancoceleste, compongono il totale delle 50 reti prese in esame.

Il capitolo Lazio conta finora 2 gol, con una rete decisiva e 2 punti attribuiti alle marcature dell’attaccante.

A incidere è il sigillo del 2-1 contro il Cagliari: sottraendo quella rete, il risultato sarebbe stato un pareggio. Da qui la differenza tra il punto della parità e i tre della vittoria.

Per Pinamonti, dunque, una rete che alimenta il bottino personale e soprattutto sposta il risultato della squadra. È proprio questo il filo conduttore della statistica: 26 volte su 50, i suoi gol hanno fatto la differenza.

#2520. Contribute to help victims of the Pedrógão Grande tragedy

Forest fires occur every summer in Portugal but the tragedy that struck the community of Pedrógão Grande in the centre of the country is unprecedented. Over 60 people lost their lives, many trapped in their cars when trying to flee from the flames.

This Just Giving page is a simple way for people, wherever they are in the world, to make a contribution to help rebuild these communities and bring some comfort to those who lost everything in the blink of an eye. The money raised will be sent to a network of local authorities and charities working in the area affected. Thank you for your support.

#2517. PSV hold on to book narrow Champions League win in Porto

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

PSV survived a second-half onslaught in Porto to secure their first win of the Champions League group stages.

Guus Til scored the only goal when he tapped in from close range after Esmir Bajraktarevic’s shot had come back off the post and was steered across goal by Sven Mijnans.

The Eindhoven club were worth their narrow lead against Francesco Farioli’s side after dominating possession in the first half, but rarely troubled Diogo Costa in the Porto goal.

Til went close with a header early in the second half before putting PSV ahead in the 57th minute.

But the visitors had to defend heroically as Porto poured forward in search of an equaliser, aided by an increasingly fragile Matej Kovar.

The goalkeeper put his defenders in jeopardy with a weak clearance in the 72nd minute, but redeemed himself by saving Victor Froholdt’s shot with his chest before Lutsarel Geertruida blocked the follow-up from Pepê.

A minute later Kovar was left stranded after he rushed out of his penalty area, but Sergiño Dest managed to hack the ball away before Alberto Costa could poke it into the open goal.

At the other end, substitute Denis Man came close to scoring a second for PSV with his first touch when his curling shot beat Costa but failed to sneak inside the post.

The 1-0 win lifts PSV to a provisional 16th place in the Champions League table at the halfway stage of round three, while Porto are still looking for their first point.

Their next match is at home to Belgian side Club Brugge, who will make the short trip to Eindhoven on November 4.

#2516. Bologna, 13 punti in sei gare con Palladino: meglio soltanto la Lazio

I rossoblù hanno vinto a San Siro contro il Milan e rimontato il Monza, interrompendo il digiuno casalingo.

Tredici punti in sei giornate: nel periodo considerato dall’arrivo di Raffaele Palladino, il Bologna avrebbe il secondo rendimento del campionato. Secondo quanto riportato da TuttoMercatoWeb, Davanti ai rossoblù ci sarebbe soltanto la Lazio. Il confronto riguarda le ultime sei gare, non la classifica complessiva di Serie A, nella quale il Bologna deve ancora recuperare terreno per avvicinarsi alle posizioni europee.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Subentrato a Domenico Tedesco sulla panchina rossoblù, Palladino ha dato continuità ai risultati di una squadra che nella prima parte della stagione aveva faticato a trovare una propria identità. I 13 punti del periodo preso in esame equivalgono a 2,16 punti a partita, un passo superato soltanto da quello della formazione biancoceleste.

Sei gare restano un intervallo limitato per formulare valutazioni definitive. Il rendimento, però, descrive il cambio di passo del Bologna e la sua maggiore capacità di raccogliere punti. Al dato si accompagnano prestazioni convincenti e successi che hanno segnato le ultime settimane dei rossoblù.

Tra le vittorie spicca il 4-2 a San Siro contro il Milan: per i rossoneri è stata fin qui l’unica sconfitta in Serie A. Il Bologna ha poi completato sabato sera la rimonta contro il Monza, mettendo fine al digiuno di successi in casa.

Il percorso di Palladino era iniziato con il pareggio contro il Torino e la sconfitta con l’Inter, risultati che non avevano portato una svolta immediata. Da Lecce in avanti, secondo la successione riportata, i rossoblù hanno raccolto quattro vittorie e un pareggio nelle cinque gare successive. Le ultime tre uscite si sono chiuse tutte con un successo.

#2515. 2. Bundesliga preview: Dynamo Dresden vs. Greuther Fürth

Dynamo Dresden will be looking to end their recent league struggles when they welcome Greuther Fürth on Friday evening, with Thomas Stamm’s side desperate for points as they look to climb away from the bottom of the 2. Bundesliga table.

The hosts head into the Matchday 11 encounter following a disappointing 2-1 defeat against Hannover 96 last weekend. Despite taking the lead through Nicolai Rapp, Dresden were unable to hold onto their advantage, with a second-half comeback leaving them bottom of the table on eight points.

Greuther Fürth, meanwhile, arrive in Saxony with renewed confidence after securing a narrow 1-0 victory over Holstein Kiel. Aaron Keller’s 87th-minute winner secured their third league victory of the campaign, moving Heiko Vogel’s side into ninth place with 12 points.

With just four points separating the two sides, Friday’s encounter presents an important opportunity for both teams to build some momentum heading into the next international break.

Dynamo Dresden have endured an inconsistent return to the second tier since promotion a few years ago and this season is no different, with two victories, two draws and six defeats from their opening ten league fixtures leaving them rooted to the foot of the table.

Stamm’s side have struggled particularly with defensive consistency, conceding 20 goals so far this season while finding the net on 12 occasions.

However, there have been encouraging signs in front of their home supporters. Dresden secured an impressive 2-0 derby victory over 1. FC Magdeburg before following that result with another 2-0 win against Schalke 04 in the DFB-Pokal.

Those performances will offer encouragement ahead of Friday’s encounter, particularly given Fürth’s own struggles for consistency this season.

The defeat against Hannover was nevertheless another frustrating setback. Dresden had looked capable of taking something from the encounter before Friedrich Müller’s dismissal allowed the hosts to take control, with the defender now unavailable for the visit of Fürth.

Stamm will therefore be demanding a response from his players, particularly in their defensive organisation and ability to maintain their intensity throughout the full 90 minutes.

Speaking ahead of the game, the Dynamo head coach acknowledged the quality of the opposition but insisted his side should approach the encounter with confidence.

“I expect a team that plays bravely with the ball and intensely against it. They resolve difficult situations with purpose. We need a really good performance, but we don’t need to hide at home.”

Stamm also stressed the importance of remaining positive, urging his players not to become frustrated if their early opportunities fail to find the back of the net.

Greuther Fürth travel to Dresden looking to build on their recent victory over Holstein Kiel, which ended a frustrating run of results and provided a welcome boost for Vogel’s side.

The visitors have collected 12 points from their opening ten league fixtures, recording three victories, three draws and four defeats. Their 13 goals scored suggest they possess some attacking quality, although 16 goals conceded highlight an area that still requires improvement.

Fürth’s biggest challenge this season has arguably been finding consistency. Their victory over Eintracht Braunschweig was followed by a disappointing 3-0 defeat against Osnabrück, before they returned to winning ways against Kiel.

The late nature of their most recent victory should nevertheless provide some confidence, with Aaron Keller finding the net in the 87th minute to secure all three points.

Vogel will be hoping his side can now register consecutive league victories for the first time this season, although he is well aware of the difficulties associated with travelling to Dresden.

The Fürth head coach has called for a better balance between defensive organisation and attacking intent, particularly after his side struggled to create opportunities in recent outings.

“Good positioning in attack is always the foundation for creating scoring opportunities. We need to be more precise in that area than we have been in recent matches.” Vogel explained ahead of the encounter.

The visitors will also be keen to avoid a repeat of their previous trip to Dresden, when they suffered a 2-0 defeat in January.

With experienced attacking players such as the league’s joint top-scorer Felix Klaus alongside the creativity of Ole Pohlmann, Fürth will certainly possess the tools to trouble their hosts if they can find the right balance going forward.

Friday’s encounter will be the 19th competitive meeting between the two sides, with Greuther Fürth holding the historical advantage.

The visitors have recorded nine victories compared to Dynamo Dresden’s four, while the remaining five encounters have ended in draws.

However, Dresden will take encouragement from their most recent meeting, which took place at the Rudolf-Harbig-Stadion in January 2026.

Goals from Thomas Keller and Jakob Lemmer secured a convincing 2-0 victory for the hosts, marking the beginning of an important run of results in their battle to secure survival last season.

Interestingly, that victory was Fürth’s only defeat in their previous nine meetings with Dresden, with the Franken side recording five victories and three draws during that period.

Vogel’s men will therefore be hoping to reassert their historical dominance, while Dresden will be looking to repeat their January performance and secure another important home victory.

Dynamo Dresden have several important absentees to contend with ahead of Friday’s fixture, particularly in the defensive department.

Friedrich Müller is suspended following his red card against Hannover, while Brooklyn Ezeh is also unavailable due to suspension following his dismissal for the club’s Under-21 side.

Lars Bünning and Kenneth Schmidt remain sidelined with longer-term injuries, while Thomas Keller continues to be unavailable following his concussion.

Luca Herrmann has also joined the injury list after picking up an injury against Hannover.

For Greuther Fürth, Maxim Dal remains sidelined through injury, although Vogel should otherwise have a relatively settled squad available.

Dynamo Dresden (4-2-3-1): Grill; Sterner, Muhamedbegovic, Rapp, Rossipal; Wagner, Raschl; Bobzien, Hauptmann, Čović; Vermeij

Greuther Fürth (4-2-3-1): Hellstern; Dehm, Heuer, Blank, Itter; Will, Bansé; Klaus, Pohlmann, Keller; Sillah

#2514. PortuGOAL.net improvements: new App and new SSL technology

As part of PortuGOAL’s efforts to constantly evolve and serve lovers of Portuguese football with top-quality and increasingly accessible content, we are replacing the PortuGOAL Android and Apple APPs with a new Progressive Web Application (PWA) as from tomorrow, Tuesday 3 April.

This big step forward in phone apps technology loads the content instantly, even in areas of low connectivity, stays up to date at all times, displays the most recent version upon launching, is more efficient than native apps, works on-demand and is always accessible, without taking up a smartphone’s valuable memory or data. This is a football site, not a technology one, but for those of you of a techy nature, click here for more on why PWAs are the future of phone applications.

In another improvement, the website itself will be implementing SSL technology at the same time, leading to more secure and faster navigation. PortuGOAListas should be directed to the new URL automatically from tomorrow, if not simply type http://portugoal.net into your browser’s address bar then bookmark the page. Feyenoord endured a frustrating evening in Spain as a penalty by Real Betis’s outstanding player, Isco, secured a 1-0 win for the La Liga side.

Isco, who was at the heart of Betis’s attacks all evening, grabbed the ball and gave Tjark Ernst no chance from the penalty spot.

Ernst had made good saves from former AZ Alkmaar striker Troy Parrott and Marc Batra to keep Feyenoord in the game in the first half, but Feyenoord had the best chance of the opening period when Gjivai Zechiël shot over the bar from close range.

The visitors had a claim for a penalty of their own turned down in the 68th minute when Givairo Read claimed he had been pushed by Ezzalzouli, but referee Simone Sozza waved the appeals away.

The result leaves Giovanni van Bronckhorst’s side in 26th place in the Champions League table, with Internazionale up next in De Kuip on November 3.

#2509. Top five football betting tips and tricks

Football is the world’s most famous sport, and it is the sport of choice of a great many sports punters as well. Especially popular in Europe, betting on football can be an incredibly profitable enterprise, so long as you know how to do it.

Getting started on football betting is far from easy, though. This form of gambling requires dedication, investment, and persistence. As you gain experience, your intuition will improve and you will be able to make more correct predictions.

But experience is not the only thing that can help you become a better football punter. In fact, you can substantially improve your betting strategy by simply following some basic football betting tips.

You might find shopping around boring and unhelpful, but you just have to do it when you search sites for football betting. As you know, the higher the odds, the bigger the payout. What you may have missed, however, is that odds are different at each bookmaker. Having said that, shopping around for the best odds is sensible, as it rewards you with bigger payouts.

In this line of thought, sticking to one bookmaker is virtually pointless. Even if the bookie of your choosing offers the best odds for a number of matches, it is highly unlikely that their odds will remain the best for long.

An obvious one, we know. And yet, you will be surprised by the number of football bettors who wager on football, but do not follow the sport. So, why is it important to follow the action closely?

First of all, by watching football matches, you can take part in the most exciting football betting market – the live betting market. What is more important, however, is that following the action provides an invaluable insight into teams and players’ style, condition, and determination – important factors  to consider when placing a bet.

As you watch more and more games, you will improve your analytic abilities. In time, you will be able to make more accurate predictions about the outcome of different matches, which, of course,  will result in placing more winning bets.

As you know, odds are based on the probability of occurrence of a certain outcome; the higher the odds, the more unlikely it is for that outcome to actually occur. But what happens when the odds offered by the bookmaker represent a probability that is lower than the actual probability of an outcome occurring? In such a situation, you can place what is called a value bet.

If you want to become a professional football punter, you should always search for value when betting, as it leads to huge profits

Favoured by beginners in particular, accumulators are a type of football wager which allows you to bet on as many fixtures as you like. Most commonly, you will be asked to choose a match winner, but as the market is diverse, you may be offered a number of other betting options. What makes accumulators an appealing betting market is the huge potential payout – here, you can stake as little as £1 and have the potential to win more than £500,000, depending on the bookmaker and the number of selected fixtures.

If you find accumulators’ payouts too good to be true, you are right. As a matter of fact, there is a reason for the odds to be set so high – actually winning an accumulator bet, especially one which involves a wide selection of matches, is nearly impossible. You see, all you need for your whole wager to lose is one wrong prediction. For example, if you bet on 10 matches and just one of them goes wrong, your entire accumulator is lost.

To make money by betting on football, avoid betting on accumulators – the risk is simply not worth it.

Finally, if you want to be a successful football punter, learn how not to mix emotions with betting. This is easier said than done, though.

Whenever your favourite team plays, you want them to win, regardless of how (im)possible that may be. If you do not learn how to leave emotions out of betting, however, you are highly likely to put money on your favourite team even if they have no chance of winning the match. Conversely, should you base your bets on hard facts and not on emotions, you increase your chances to win.

Our advice – avoid betting on fixtures that involve one of your favourite teams, that is the easiest way to keep emotions at bay.

#2508. Serie A, domenica Inter-Como: tutto quello che c'è da sapere sulla squadra di Fabregas

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Dopo la vittoria in Champions League in casa del Feyenoord, la seconda stagionale nella League Phase, l’Inter si prepara al prossimo impegno in campionato, l’ultimo prima della sosta per gli impegni delle Nazionali, con i nerazzurri che ospiteranno a San Siro il Como nell’11ª giornata di Serie A. Un confronto che nella scorsa stagione ha regalato gol e spettacolo, una sfida tra due squadre ricche di qualità: l’Inter occupa il secondo posto in classifica a quota 25 punti, con il secondo miglior attacco del campionato con 26 gol segnati; il Como, quarto, ha conquistato 23 punti e ha la miglior difesa del torneo al pari della Lazio, con otto reti subite. La squadra di Fabregas, in settimana, ha conquistato una roboante vittoria per 1-9 in Champions League in casa del Lens, e sta vivendo un avvio di stagione da protagonista sia in Italia che in Europa.

Arrivato alla sua quarta stagione sulla panchina lariana, Fabregas è riuscito a costruire un sistema di gioco riconoscibile e cucito sul talento dei suoi giocatori offensivi, riuscendo allo stesso tempo a dare alla squadra equilibrio in fase difensiva. Il tecnico catalano ha impostato un 4-2-3-1 in grado di far coesistere contemporaneamente in campo almeno quattro giocatori offensivi, sfruttandone anche l’atletismo e la predisposizione al ripiegamento per tenere le giuste distanze in campo. Il Como possiede grandi qualità nella gestione del possesso, che sfrutta per chiudere gli avversari nella propria metà campo e creare presupposti per occasioni da gol: nelle prime dieci giornate, il dato del possesso palla medio dei lariani è del 62,1%, il secondo in Serie A alle spalle dell’Inter (62,4%). Una mole di gioco che, in proporzione, non ha prodotto un elevato numero di situazioni da rete: con 18 gol segnati in 10 partite il Como è il sesto attacco del campionato, un dato in linea con quello degli XG (18.32).

La squadra di Fabregas riesce a costruire le sue manovre offensive attraverso due sviluppi principali: le corsie esterne, presidiate da due terzini costantemente proiettati in avanti, capaci di arrivare al cross con grande qualità e di combinare con i trequartisti, e le combinazioni nello stretto nella zona della trequarti avversaria. Il Como è molto pericoloso quando riesce a mettere un giocatore nelle condizioni di crossare con spazio, grazie alla grande capacità dei giocatori offensivi di riempire l’area di rigore (almeno tre considerando la punta, il trequartista della fascia opposta e l’inserimento di uno dei due mediani). Allo stesso modo, la duttilità e l’intelligenza nell’occupare gli spazi e le posizioni intermedie di giocatori come Nico Paz, Baturina, Da Cunha consente al Como di creare superiorità numerica sulla trequarti o all’ingresso dell’area di rigore: diversi giocatori in zona palla in grado di dar vita a combinazioni nello stretto e tentare la conclusione. Proprio le qualità balistiche dei trequartisti del Como rappresenta un’ulteriore arma offensiva: i lariani hanno tentato 203 conclusioni nelle prime dieci giornate (secondo miglior dato alle spalle delle 220 tentate dall’Inter).

Difensivamente, invece, il Como cerca di aggredire gli avversari nella propria metà campo in diverse fasi del match, arrivando a realizzare quattro reti da situazione di recupero palla alto. Un tipo di pressione che si è rivelata efficace, ma che ha anche lasciato campo e occasioni agli avversari una volta superata. A sua volta, poi, la squadra di Fabregas ha incontrato qualche difficoltà contro squadre capaci di aggredire alte sulla costruzione dal basso (Torino, Feyenoord), o contro squadre abili a ribaltare velocemente il fronte una volta recuperato il pallone.

Richiamando la storia antifascista del quartiere, ha affermato che la comunità «non indietreggerà di un solo passo».

#2504. Bosz frustrated as Feyenoord hold PSV to 1-1 draw in Eindhoven

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

Feyenoord preserved their four-point advantage over champions PSV after coming away with a point from Eindhoven thanks to an improved second-half performance.

PSV dominated the first half as they deployed a close-passing, tiki-taka style to stifle Feyenoord, but only had Guus Til’s well-taken volley after 20 minutes to show for it.

Ten minutes after the break Feyenoord equalised when Anis Hadj Moussa was given too much space to charge down the right flank and bend a shot inside the far post.

The Algerian had a good chance for a second towards the end, while PSV goalkeeper Matej Kovar had to be alert to keep out a header by Nacho Ferri from close range.

“We were by far the better team today and had the better chances,” said PSV coach Peter Bosz. “We started the second half badly and made a mistake, after which Hadj Moussa scored. And even then we could have won it.”

Ajax overtook PSV to close the gap at the top to three points with a hard-fought win at Sparta. Veteran striker Jens Toornstra scored for Sparta on his 500th Eredivisie appearance, but Abdellah Ouazane and Tolu Arokodare, the scorers against Atalanta in midweek, were on target again to claim the win for Ajax.

Twente moved up to fourth place as two late goals by Sondre Ørjasæter and Lucas Vennegoor of Hesselink earned a 2-0 win at AZ Alkmaar, who like Twente and PSV have 20 points.

Fortuna Sittard also snatched the points late on against Excelsior as Rodrigo Guth headed in a corner in injury time to keep the Limburg club in sixth place, two points further back.

At the other end of the table, the return of Adrie Koster as veteran coach brought no relief to Willem II, who crashed to a 6-1 home defeat to Cambuur.

The Tilburgers remain four points behind ADO Den Haag, who were unable to keep up the momentum of last week’s win against PSV as they lost 3-2 at home to Heerenveen.

Telstar lost by the same margin at Go Ahead Eagles, whose striker Søren Tengstedt netted twice in the first half to lead the Eredivisie scoring charts with eight goals.

#2502. Brocchi: "La Lazio di Gattuso somiglia alla mia. Dove può arrivare? Vi dico che..."

Il primato in classifica e il momento straordinario che sta attraversando la Lazio di Gattuso non sono passati inosservati agli ex biancocelesti. In particolare, a parlarne è stato Cristian Brocchi sulle colonne de La Repubblica. L'ex centrocampista biancoceleste, protagonista della Lazio del 2010 di Reja, visse un inizio campionato molto simile a quello dell'attuale formazione laziale. Di seguito l'intervista.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

L’entusiasmo con cui questa squadra scende in campo, è lo stesso che avevamo noi. E anche allora eravamo reduci da una stagione difficile e sofferta, siamo stati capaci di trasformare negatività e scetticismo in energia positiva, proprio come questa Lazio. Nessuno voleva rivivere le sensazioni dell’anno prima. E poi il mercato. Nel gennaio precedente, parliamo del 2010, arrivarono giocatori importanti come Biava, Dias e Floccari, che si integrarono subito e sistemarono la squadra. Così siamo partiti, pochi mesi dopo, con uno spirito diverso e più certezze. Stessa cosa è successa con Taylor: arrivato a gennaio 2026, è uno dei perni della formazione di Gattuso, un grande calciatore.

Si rivede in qualche centrocampista della Lazio di oggi? Stimo molto Frattesi, che ho allenato al Monza. Dico sempre che dentro ha il 50% di Brocchi, cioè la mentalità vincente e la voglia di lottare, l’altro 50% è l’abilità in zona gol, che io non avevo. In tv, a inizio campionato, avevo detto che avrebbe potuto competere per l’Europa, perché ha giocatori di valore con caratteristiche adatte al calcio di Gattuso. Lui ha sempre creduto in questo gioco aggressivo, non è cambiato. Però ora non alziamo troppo l’asticella, a Roma è pericoloso, lo so bene, poi bastano un paio di passi falsi e subito si dice che è finita la magia o la benzina. Si passa dall’euforia allo sconforto in un attimo. È successo anche a noi, quando perdemmo il primo posto dopo le sconfitte nel derby e a Cesena. E a gennaio ricordo le polemiche perché ci si aspettava più rinforzi sul mercato (arrivò solo Sculli, ndr). Bisogna procedere partita dopo partita, con realismo, e reagire bene ai momenti negativi.

Obiettivi? Per come si sta esprimendo adesso, anche questa squadra può lottare per entrare tra le prime quattro: avevo previsto che si sarebbe classificata tra la sesta e l’ottava posizione, ora dico tra la quarta e la sesta. Il percorso però è lungo, le big sono più abituate a fare punti pesanti, impossibile sottovalutarle. E ci saranno tanti scontri diretti da affrontare. Ma se la Lazio sarà così in alto anche alla vigilia degli ultimi due mesi, allora tutto potrà succedere.

Gattuso? Ha rivitalizzato calciatori forti che non avevano reso secondo il loro valore. Penso in particolare a Tavares e Zaccagni: con Sarri, che considero comunque un grande allenatore, erano come leoni in gabbia, Rino li ha liberati e ora fanno la differenza. Anche Noslin, Isaksen e lo stesso Taylor rendono molto di più. Provstgaard non giocava, adesso è un titolare.

Ha un sogno, Gattuso? Conoscendolo, vorrebbe rivedere l’Olimpico pieno. Io sono molto legato al popolo biancoceleste, è fantastico: quando firmai per la Lazio ero felice perché da avversario ero incantato dall’atmosfera di quello stadio e finalmente sarebbe stato il mio campo. Quindi capisco il sogno di Rino, spero riesca a realizzarlo.

#2500. Freiburg end Thomas Reis’ winning streak at Trabzonspor by stopping Salah in battling away victory

SC Freiburg earned a hard-fought win in their UEFA Europa Conference League trip to Trabzonspor. Julian Schuster's side are now level on points with their hosts after ending Thomas Reis’ seven-game winning start at the Turkish side.

Despite the electric atmosphere from the home fans, Freiburg were calm in possession from the first whistle. They thoroughly deserved the opening goal that arrived in the 14th minute from a set-piece opportunity.

André Onana saved Igor Matanović's powerful header from Vincenzo Grifo's corner-kick delivery, but the rebound fell kindly to Lukas Kübler as the all-rounder scored his fifth European goal with a simple finish.

The Cameroonian goalkeeper was at fault for Freiburg's next chance, although Matanović left him off the hook with a weak effort. Trabzonspor had the first real chance with Ernest Muçi's effort from outside the box moments after Mohamed Salah's deflected free-kick.

Freiburg threatened to double their lead from set-piece situations as Batista Mendy made a crucial block to deny the captain Maximilian Eggestein before Onana dealt with Grifo's direct free-kick. Salah had his first open play chance before the half-time break, but his effort was wide off the mark. At the other end, Grifo's customary shot similarly failed to hit the target before Cyriaque Irié's unsuccessful header.

The home fans put the referee Kristo Tohvar under pressure early after the restart as he waved play to continue following an incident inside Freiburg's box involving Paul Onuachu and Kübler. The Nigerian striker then created a big chance for Salah, but Mio Backhaus made his first big save from close range.

For the first time in the game, the Bundesliga side were under immense pressure with all the action restricted to one half of the pitch. However, they managed to weather Trabzon's second-half storm as Backhaus and his defenders dealt with several crosses into the box. Thanks to their first clean sheet since matchday 3 in Bundesliga, last season's Europa League finalists have now won three consecutive games in different competitions.

Freiburg will host Union Berlin before the international break as they hope to stay in the top three of the Bundesliga. Their remaining Europa Conference League games will see them face Twente (H), Panathinaikos (H) and Kauno Zalgiris (A).

#2499. Sports club members becoming less tolerant of homosexuality

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

Acceptance of homosexuality in Dutch sports clubs has declined significantly in the last generation, according to a survey of attitudes by the Mulier Institute.

The organisation found that 66% of people had no objection to a fellow member of a sports club being gay, while in 2008 the figure was 85%.

Just over half (55%) of respondents said they had no problem sharing a shower with a gay team-mate of the same sex, which also represented a drop from 73% in 2008.

The Mulier Institute said the findings reflected a social trend that has also been seen in schools, where the proportion of boys aged between 12 and 16 with a positive view of homosexuality has dropped from 40% to 22% since 2021.

The Dutch Olympic Committee NOC*NSF said the findings were “highly regrettable” and pledged to step up its efforts to make sport more inclusive.

But the John Blankenstein Foundation, which campaigns for LGBT emancipation in sport, said it had noticed declining enthusiasm for its workshops and growing numbers of parents who voiced objections.

Karin Blankenstein, who set up the organisation in 2008, told NOS: “I get the sensitivity around transgender people in sport at the top level, but this is about amateur sport.”

#2496. Opinion: The morality of football

Yesterday evening, Selecao correspondent Nathan Motz accepted a unique opportunity to appear as a guest on the Moral Maze – a BBC radio broadcast dealing with matters of moral and ethical significance.

Is football a moral force for good? That was the question of the day, and each of the four guests were asked to discuss various aspects of the debate from financial fairplay to club takeovers by foreign entities to racism.

Nathan’s segment was concerned with whether football has transcendent moral value, and what that is exactly. Is there a greater significance to the game we are watching, or is it merely 22 humans kicking a leather ball into a net?

Join Nathan as he elaborates on the most significant takeaways of what was a fascinating discussion on the sport and morality in general.

Football, for some, may be the most poignant reminder of the moral universe in which we live. While debate has raged for centuries regarding the precise origin of moral absolutes and how humans ought to live, it is simply unavoidable to say humanity is often troubled by events surrounding the game we all love so dearly.

In not so distant history, every normal act of human life would have been assigned some degree of moral significance. Every action had inherently moral indicators - for good or for evil. Today, postmodern views of morality dominate. These describe morality as an entirely subjective dimension. In other words, it cannot be pinned down exactly whether behaviors are objectively good or bad. We can only find fault with someone else’s interpretation based on their own unique moral code. Morality is relative to the individual or society.

Yet last night’s dialogue concerning football and human behavior was filled with moral accusations and claims which strongly suggested humans are not content with relativist morality. Indeed, we are at times frightened by and disgusted with what we observe in and around football. Our sense of moral justice is deeply disturbed. But with respect to what exactly?

In responding to the panelist’s questions, I observed a familiar paradigm. Each time I have discussed any aspect of moral behavior, the speculation humans are good or at worst morally neutral animals continually reemerges. This widely accepted presupposition is then followed shortly by disgust at some aspect of human behavior. Almost as if to ask “why are all these good humans behaving so badly?” while ignoring the very real implications of this line of inquiry. A question rendered unanswerable by the insistent a priori assumptions from which it was derived.

If humans are intrinsically beneficent, conscientious, fair, humble, and honest then why is football rife with racism, misogyny, greed, disenfranchisement, lies, cheating, violence, and the hypocrisy to deny the occurrence of any such behaviors except when a rival club is the guilty party? Behaviors which are perpetrated by individual supporters, fanatical groups, club administrations, corporate bodies such as FIFA, political entities, nations, and even the players themselves. No one participating faction is guiltless.

To so blithely sidestep more sufficient explanations for the empirical reality of wickedness in football and in human behavior more generally is not just factually incorrect. It undermines real attempts to understand the root of these behaviors where they might be dealt with more effectively.

To listen to The Moral Maze episode entitled “Football: a moral force for good?” produced by BBC radio and featuring PortuGOAL’s Seleção correspondent Nathan Motz click on this link

Furthermore, in good faith, how successful is a response against heinous evil when those perpetrating it are regarded as behaving in accord with their unique moral ambitions? Humans refuse to accept their indisputably objective moral lacking at a fundamental level. This restricts us from ever doing any more than “stamping out” the alleged impropriety of others – while we ourselves may readily engage in these very same behaviors. Moral relativism cannot account for the moral outrage emanating from all corners of the football world. Is Qatar wrong to perpetrate human rights atrocities or is it allowable because their culture is indifferent? Is hooliganism objectively wrong, or only when someone else’s club is responsible?

The overlooking of our own failings while lashing out at others for theirs is quintessential to the morally fallen nature of humanity itself. Football tells all. There are not good people and bad people. There are only people who both willfully and unintentionally transgress the moral code embedded in each of our hearts. A moral law which can only have ever been given by a moral Lawgiver. Otherwise, it is truly your word against mine as it pertains to the moral validity of all behaviors - even those which may deeply violate your conscience.

Football generates an episodic, semantic, and imagistic description of universal archetypes: good and evil, sacrifice and greed, cruelty and mercy, hero and anti-hero. It speaks to every arc of life’s plotline: tragedy, comedy, victory, defeat, the finite nature of our circumstances. It is one of the preeminent existential dialects alongside religion, visual art, written and spoken language, music, and theatre. Football plucks a primal chord.

As metaphysics have been curtailed to just physics in the modern secular West, football has only elevated our awareness of the often dire moral predicaments of our species. Yet it is clear the moral conversation does not originate on a football pitch. The pitch is but a grand stage upon which moral themes may be observed and critiqued, a mirror held in front of our own soul.

The desire for ultimate purpose, for meaning beyond the material realm, never disappeared in modernity. Arguably, it has only become more desperate as the outcry for ultimate significance comes under more scrutiny and criticism by those who constrain ontological explanations to those accounted for by physics. The severity of the mental health crisis in the West is in no small part explained by the terrifying existential awareness we are cut adrift in an abstract sea of meaningless, hopeless wandering between two oblivions - that before mortal life began and that at mortal life’s end. Let football be one voice which reassures you - there is ultimate meaning and good reasons why football so unsettles our craving for justice.

In a palpable, unavoidable way, football and supporter communities across Europe are encountering moral quandaries. In Portugal, age-old debates between FC Porto and Benfica have routinely been haunted by, in many cases, farcical and cynical hypocrisy. Throughout its history, the FPF has been susceptible to showing partiality to a club, player, or style which ultimately degrades the performance of our beloved Seleção. Deny it all we like, and not one aspect of this reality is changed.

In conclusion, whatever your thoughts or beliefs on the subject of moral significance, its origin, the meaning of life, and how football draws that out, I hope it will be beneficial to examine these matters more deeply. In my zeal, I admit I have not always been prudent to initiate these discussions in a way which respects thoughtful disagreement. It is my hope this effort to bring that discussion to Portuguese football will be well-received and carefully pondered. But I will contentedly listen to those followers who may be genuinely unhappy or unsupportive of this or any other idea about which I write.

The readers of this website and the larger Portuguese football loving world are my people. It is a joy and a responsibility I take seriously whenever I have the opportunity to raise important questions about the Selecao, the game, and our lives in general.

#2491. Quote Maggiorate Inter, le migliori quote promo sulla prossima partita

Quote Maggiorate Inter, le migliori quote promo sulla prossima partita

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Cosa si intende per quota maggiorata? Come funzionano le quote maggiorate? In che circostanze questa promo è messa a disposizione degli utenti dei migliori siti scommesse con licenza ADM? Noi della redazione di FC Inter 1908 abbiamo analizzato le migliori quote maggiorate oggi Inter per Serie A, Champions League e Coppa Italia.

Capitolo a parte meritano le Quote Maggiorate Inter Antepost, che riguardano le possibilità di vittoria dei nerazzurri nelle competizioni alle quali partecipano nel corso della stagione sportiva. La formazione nerazzurra ha rinforzato sul mercato la rosa che ha vinto Scudetto e Coppa Italia nella stessa stagione, confermando in primis il tecnico Chivu.

L'annata 2025/26 si è rivelata una stagione da incorniciare per gli uomini di Christian Chivu, che intendono ripetersi anche in questo campionato di Serie A, con l'obiettivo parallelo di compiere il cammino più lungo possibile in Champions League e Coppa Italia.

Domenica 8 novembre alle 18:00 va in scena a San Siro il big match tra Inter e Como valido per l'11° giornata di Serie A. I nerazzurri attualmente occupano la seconda posizione in classifica a tre lunghezze dalla Lazio capolista. In questo spazio, noi di FC Inter 1908 siamo soliti raccogliere le migliori quote maggiorate Inter, aggiornate quotidianamente dai migliori bookmaker con licenza ADM.

Non solo Inter nel panorama delle quote maggiorate di oggi, ma tanti altri eventi ed incontri da segnalare su cui i migliori siti scommesse con licenza ADM hanno scelto di offrire una maggiorazione. Si tratta di una pratica molto diffusa tra gli operatori e, per questo, quotidianamente noi di FCInter1908 saremo pronti ad aggiornarvi con le ultimissime novità nel presente articolo. Il nostro consiglio è sempre quello di tener d'occhio i T&C delle offerte, per trovare quella più adatta alle proprie esigenze e verificare a priori se si possiedono tutti i requisiti per accedervi.

Per comprendere al meglio come funziona la quota maggiorata oggi sull’Inter e, in generale, il funzionamento delle quote maggiorate nei siti scommesse con bonus scommesse di benvenuto, è bene partire dalle basi. Si definiscono quote maggiorate quelle quote che subiscono una maggiorazione rispetto al loro valore di base.

Facciamo un esempio: in Inter-Torino il segno 1 viene dato a 1,45, ma l’operatore stabilisce di fornire un bonus quota maggiorata su questo match e dunque il segno 1 viene dato a 6,00. In questo modo, le probabilità che l’Inter batta il Torino rimangono le stesse, ma ad aumentare è l’eventuale vincita di chi scommette sul segno 1.

Ricordiamo di visionare sempre attentamente i termini e condizioni delle quote maggiorate, i quali contengono tutti i requisiti da rispettare – di puntata, scadenze ed eventuali limitazioni.

Dopo aver illustrato come funzionano le quote maggiorate sull’Inter - ma anche sulle altre squadre - è bene specificare chi può scommettere sulle quote maggiorate offerte dai bookmakers. Ebbene, gli operatori mettono a disposizione questo particolare bonus scommesse per tutti i possessori di un conto gioco attivo e verificato. Alcuni utilizzano una quota maggiorata come bonus benvenuto, ma ciò non priva la possibilità agli utenti già iscritti di misurarsi con altri bonus di questo tipo all’interno delle aree del sito dedicate.

La posizione delle quote maggiorate sul sito scommesse su cui si possiede un conto gioco attivo dipende dal bookmaker stesso. Nella maggior parte dei casi, la posizione scelta per mostrare una quota maggiorata è il banner posto in alto nella Home Page oppure le primissime posizioni del menù principale.

Vi sono, tuttavia, anche scelte diverse da parte di altri operatori che inseriscono le quote maggiorate nella sezione “Bonus” o “Promozioni”, oppure assegnano uno o più bonus quota maggiorata per ciascuno degli eventi in programma – e sono dunque selezionabili direttamente cliccando sull’evento da inserire in schedina. Da non sottovalutare, inoltre, le quote maggiorate come bonus ricorrenti, ossia inviate di tanto in tanto via mail o notifica push agli utenti in possesso di determinati requisiti.

#2488. Dual passports issue continues to plague Dutch-based footballers

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

Dutch-born footballers who were called up to play for former colonies such as Suriname and Indonesia are still in limbo over their national status, six months after the “passportgate” crisis first flared up.

Suriname’s parliament passed a law in September enabling players who are selected for the national team to gain citizenship automatically.

But the Dutch immigration service IND said the law passed in Suriname made no difference to the rules on dual nationality in the Netherlands.

“The fact that Surinamese nationality has been obtained by legal means is not enough on its own to qualify for one of the exceptional categories under our law,” a spokesman for the Dutch justice ministry told broadcaster NOS.

The law was intended to settle the question of dual nationality for footballers, which arose last March when NAC Breda challenged the eligibility of Go Ahead Eagles defender Dean James.

James took Indonesian citizenship last year after accepting an invitation to play for the national team, but under Dutch dual nationality rules he was required to give up his original passport.

The Dutch football association KNVB launched an investigation into the employment status of players who were called up by countries such as Suriname, Cape Verde and Indonesia. In the meantime it arranged for Dutch-based players to be given temporary work permits so they could keep playing for their clubs.

Losing Dutch nationality also stripped them of their EU citizenship, meaning they would need to obtain visas and meet stricter criteria, such as an annual salary threshold of €600,000, to be eligible to play in the Eredivisie.

Several players have passed up invitations to join the Surinamese squad because of the uncertainty, including Danilho Doekhi of Lazio, Milan van Ewijk of Coventry City and Jayden Oosterwolde of Fenerbahçe.

National team manager Henk Fraser said it was “inexplicable” that players’ livelihoods were being damaged by their desire to play international football.

“Both countries have to get together and clarify the situation,” he told NOS. “This is a straightforward case of people following their hearts and wanting to play for Suriname, but not knowing where they stand.”

Some players said they felt misled by the Surinamese football association, which told them they were playing for the national team on “sports passports” that allowed them to remain Dutch citizens.

Former Feyenoord winger Jean-Paul Boëtius said he only discovered after he was first called up for Suriname last year that he had inadvertently renounced his Dutch citizenship as a result.

Shortly afterwards he received a letter from the city council in Rotterdam telling him to hand in his Dutch passport, which he had recently renewed, because it was no longer valid.

“I now have a sticker in my Surinamese passport that says I have a residency permit allowing me to live in the Netherlands,” he told NOS. “But that’s only for a year. It’s a very difficult situation. I want to become Dutch again. I was born and brought up here.”

#2484. Feyenoord facing fireworks fine after 3-0 loss to Internazionale

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

Feyenoord are facing a heavy fine from European football authorities after fireworks were let off during their Champions League defeat by Internazionale.

Referee Glenn Nyberg suspended the match for several minutes after flares were fired onto the roof from outside De Kuip stadium, damaging the structure. Police later made two arrests.

“It’s obviously not right and it’ll be another fine,” coach Giovanni van Bronckhorst said wearily after the game, which ended in a comprehensive 3-0 defeat for his team.

One flare landed on the pitch despite the club taking measures such as hanging nets from the gantries to stop objects being thrown from the stands. “They’re letting off fireworks from outside now,” Van Bronckhorst said. “What do you do about that?”

Uefa are likely to hit Feyenoord with a fine and could order the club to close parts of the stadium for their next home matches in the competition, against Porto on November 24 and RB Leipzig on January 27.

The club also have a suspended penalty hanging over them from last October, when they were fined €60,000 after fans let off fireworks during the match against Greek club Panathinaikos.

On the pitch, Feyenoord were undone by a classic display of Italian defending and counter-attacking, as Internazionale sat back after taking an eighth-minute lead through Yann Bisseck.

Francesco Pio Esposito headed in a second early in the second half, just as Feyenoord were pushing for an equaliser, before Piotr Zielinski made sure of the win with a shot from 20 metres.

Captain Luciano Valente said the result was “more than deserved” and the visitors had shown more “quality”.

“This defeat was harder to take than the one against Barcelona,” he told Ziggo Sport. “Because we thought going in that we could get something, but we just weren’t good enough.”

Van Bronckhorst said he was not surprised by the difference in class between the teams. “They scored the first goal out of nothing,” he said. “And Inter know better what the game needs at every stage.

“Their players have a lot of experience, they’ve been in the final twice in the last four years. So we’re at a disadvantage.”

#2483. Pancaro: “L'unica cosa che non deve fare la Lazio è quella di mettersi pressione”

Dall'avvio da record alla gestione della pressione: l'analisi di Giuseppe Pancaro sulla straordinaria partenza biancoceleste.

Giuseppe Pancaro, intervenuto ai microfoni di Radiosei, ha analizzato il super momento della Lazio: la forza del gruppo, i successi senza pubblico e la gestione saggia degli obiettivi futuri.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Come sto vivendo questo momento Lazio? E' una Lazio bellissima, non è un momento. Ventotto punti in 10 gare non è un caso. C'è del valore, grande merito da parte di Gattuso. Finalmente anche la soddisfazione per i tifosi che da anni non sognavano più e adesso stanno gioendo.

Ancora più sorprendente fare questi risultati senza tifosi all'Olimpico? Questo è un ulteriore merito di questo gruppo perché la difficoltà ambientale è oggettiva da anni. La sensazione è che loro ora non avvertano questo problema. Sicuramente giocare una gara come quella col Cagliari davanti a 13000 spettatori rispetto ad uno stadio pieno qualcosa toglie sempre. Questa squadra è talmente forte, concentrata, unita e compatta che riesce ad andare oltre.

C'è un momento in ogni stagione in cui ci si accorge che l'obiettivo postosi è cambiato. Quale sarà questo momento in cui magari dai 40 punti si passa a pensare ad una coppa europea? Gattuso in tal senso è bravissimo. L'unica cosa che non deve fare la Lazio è quella di mettersi pressione. Eventualmente penso se ne possa parlare in primavera, al momento fanno benissimo a giocare pensando ad una gara alla volta senza mettersi addosso pressione.

#2482. Garcia's error costs Leverkusen as Gouiri gifts Olympique Marseille victory

Bayer Leverkusen's flawless start to their Europa League campaign came unstuck after a 1-0 defeat to Olympique Marseille tonight. In a game that truly failed to ever ignite, Amine Gouiri's 77th-minute strike sealed victory for the club from the south coast of France.

In what proceeded to be a rather tame opening half, Marseille were first to show signs of life. Amine Gouiri slipped the ball through Jarell Quansah's legs, but failed to get the better of Mark Flekken. Otherwise, chances were limited for both sides who failed to land punches.

Leverkusen found trouble in the final third, looking incapable of getting into threatening positions. The Rhinelanders required all of 28 minutes before Patrik Schick set up Quansah. However, the centre-half could only muster up a defender's finish which cleared the crossbar.

Ibrahim Maza soon found room for a central shot that failed to trouble Jeffrey de Lange. Had it gone in, the offside flag was already raised against Schick. The hosts were denied by the width of the bar before the break as Miguel Gutierrez's hit took a nasty deflection.

The second-half took on a similar life to the first until Igor Paixão and Gouiri unfurled shots that kept Flekken on his toes. Leverkusen's dominance prior to the interval appeared to have been relinquished as Jeoffrey Kondogbia was inches away from breaking the deadlock.

A goal eventually arrived, falling the way of the Ligue 1 side with 13 minutes remaining. Aleix Garcia will wince watching it back, letting the ball get away from him. Gouiri proceeded to race free and cut in before bending a left-footed shot beyond Flekken's despairing fingertips.

Leverkusen's sense of urgency ramped up as Edmond Tapsoba misfired from a corner. De Lange flapped at it, but made amends at the death by holding onto Schick's stoppage time header. A result which sealed a precious first victory in Marseille's Europa League odyssey.

#2481. PSV edged out by sharper Club Brugge in Champions League defeat

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

See more DutchNews articles in your Google search results

PSV Eindhoven endured a frustrating night in the Champions League as their defensive frailties were exposed in a 2-1 defeat by Club Brugge.

PSV started full of attacking intent and should have taken an early lead when Ivan Perisic put Sergiño Dest clean through on goal, but the defender’s shot was too close to goalkeeper Yann Sommer.

Instead it was the visitors who took the lead with their first real attack when Nicolo Tresoldi headed in a teasing cross from Joaquin Seys.

PSV equalised early in the second half as an outstanding pass by Sven Mijnans into the path Lutsharel Geertruida allowed the defender to charge to the byline. His pullback found its way to Kodai Sano, whose shot took a deflection off Seys into the net.

But five minutes later PSV were caught out as another razor-sharp pass by Seys released Carlos Forbs in space to net the winner.

The home side refused to give up, but were unable to find a way past Sommer in the closing stages as the Belgian side closed out the win.

Team captain Mauro Junior admitted that PSV had been undone by shortcomings at the back. “We had a lot of opportunities in the first half but we didn’t score,” he told Ziggo Sport.

“We want to attack and score goals, but we weren’ well organised today in defence. We need to keep our heads up and look forward to the next game.”

The result leaves PSV in 24th place, just above the elimination line, ahead of their next match away to Real Madrid on November 24, with four points from their opening four matches.

#2480. Lazio, Liverani frena sullo scudetto: Gattuso pensa gara per gara

L’ex centrocampista guarda alla trasferta di Napoli: una buona prestazione darebbe entusiasmo durante la sosta.

La Lazio è prima da sola, ma Fabio Liverani invita a non parlare ancora di scudetto. Secondo quanto riportato da Il Messaggero, L’ex centrocampista biancoceleste considera la qualificazione in Champions League un grande risultato e condivide la scelta di Rino Gattuso di affrontare una partita alla volta. In collegamento con Radio Sei, durante la trasmissione Quelli che hanno portato il calcio a Roma, ha parlato anche della prossima sfida con il Napoli, della vittoria sul Cagliari e di Belahyane e Isaksen.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La trasferta di Napoli è, per Liverani, un’occasione per arrivare alla sosta con ulteriore entusiasmo. Conta il risultato, ma conta anche la prova della squadra: «Fare bene a Napoli sarebbe la ciliegina sulla torta. Un risultato positivo, ma soprattutto una prestazione positiva, permetterebbe di lavorare durante la sosta con grande entusiasmo». L’ex biancoceleste ricorda che la Lazio raggiungerà comunque la pausa da capolista, indipendentemente dall’esito della gara.

Sulle ambizioni stagionali, invece, il suo auspicio è preciso: «Auguro alla Lazio di arrivare in Champions League. Parlare di scudetto è difficile, ma anche la Champions, considerando quello che si diceva in estate, sarebbe un grande risultato». Liverani richiama la lunghezza del campionato e le insidie ancora da affrontare. Per questo approva l’approccio di Gattuso, concentrato su una gara alla volta: a suo giudizio, il tecnico ha costruito così il percorso della squadra.

Liverani torna poi sul successo contro il Cagliari, arrivato nella terza partita in sette giorni. La Lazio, osserva, non è stata brillante. Proprio per questo dà valore al risultato: «Vincere una gara del genere ha un significato importante, perché dimostra che questa squadra ha raggiunto una certa maturità».

Secondo l’ex centrocampista, la posizione in classifica porta anche una maggiore attenzione da parte delle avversarie, che proveranno a contenere i principali pericoli biancocelesti. Cita il Cagliari e la scelta di schierare Fadera. La vittoria, nella sua lettura, è stata diversa dalle altre: la Lazio è riuscita a portarla a casa pur incontrando difficoltà.

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 10 of the Eredivisie ...

Xavi Hernandez has his first win as Netherlands head coach as a Mexx Meerdink double led Oranje to a 2-1 win over Serbia in the Nations League.

Xavi made two changes from the side that drew with Germany as Mexx Meerdink replaced the injured Brian Brobbey up front while Joey Veerman came in for Quinten Timber.

Netherlands dominated from the start but they were forced into an early change when Cody Gakpo suffered a knock and Ruben van Bommel came on.

After half an hour, Crysencio Summerville was clipped in the box and Meerdink made it 1-0 from the penalty spot. It was almost 2-0 before the break but Denzel Dumfries could not convert when through on goal.

Serbia made a double change at the break with Luka Jovic and Dusan Tadic coming on. A minute into the second half, the change worked as Jovic was given space in the box to net after Netherlands failed to clear the ball.

Netherlands went in search of the lead again but Tijjani Reijnders blazed a huge chance over while Ruben van Bommel hit the post. Meerdink did eventually make it 2-1 with a lovely finish after a quick 1-2 with substitute Guus Til.

There was to be no hattrick for Meerdink as he was brought off and Netherlands played the final ten minutes with Til through the middle.

Serbia pressed for a late equaliser but the Netherlands had a golden chance to make it 3-1 when Til found himself in on goal. He had Summerville and Van Bommel to his left but overhit the pass and the later’s finish was too weak to find the net.

The Netherlands held on for the victory which takes them to the top of the group with a clash against Greece next on Thursday.

It's time for our BIG Dutch Eredivisie season preview! Football-Oranje's ...

A new season of Dutch football is here! Football-Oranje's Michael ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 11 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 10 of the Eredivisie ...

An injury-hit Netherlands side battled back to draw 2-2 against Greece in the Nations League.

Xavi was forced into changes with Cody Gakpo and Jan Paul van Hecke dropping out of the squad. Tijjani Reijnders and Ryan Gravenberch also dropped to the bench with Quinten Timber, Guus Til, Jurrien Timber and Ruben van Bommel starting.

Greece have been in good form recently and their confidence was clear from the start. They had a goal disallowed for offside before Fotis Ioannidis made it 1-0 after 23 minutes.

The Netherlands offered little going forward and in stoppage time, a bad back pass from Van Bommel led to Giorgos Masouras sliding in a second.

Xavi made a triple change as Gravenberch, Noa Lang and Reijnders came on and they all made a big difference as the Netherlands dominated the second half.

Virgil van Dijk headed one back before Denzel Dumfries had two goals disallowed for offside. Xavi turned to Joshua Zirkzee and debutante Gjivai Zechiel in hope of an equaliser.

The equaliser came in the last minute as Zechiel’s overhead kick dropped into the path of Reijnders who blasted the ball in. Zechiel then came close to a winner but his header was straight at the goalkeeper.

A battling point for the Netherlands, who stay unbeaten under Xavi with a game at home to Serbia to come on Sunday.

#2476. Taylor, la Juventus torna alla carica: la Lazio chiede 60 milioni

Per il centrocampista olandese, già cercato in estate, l'ipotesi di offerta bianconera si aggira sui 40 milioni.

La Juventus sarebbe tornata sulle tracce di Taylor, centrocampista olandese della Lazio, e starebbe valutando un’offerta vicina ai 40 milioni di euro. Secondo quanto riportato da Calciomercato, Claudio Lotito, però, non avrebbe intenzione di cederlo per meno di 60 milioni. Il club bianconero vuole rafforzare il centrocampo già da gennaio.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Secondo quanto raccolto da Calciomercato.it, la Juventus aveva cercato Taylor con insistenza già durante la scorsa estate. Il giocatore, classe 2002, è tornato nel mirino dei bianconeri come possibile rinforzo per il centrocampo.

Taylor è tra gli uomini più importanti della formazione allenata da Gennaro Gattuso. La Lazio è prima in classifica dopo le prime dieci giornate, nelle quali ha ottenuto nove vittorie e un pareggio. L’interesse juventino riguarda dunque uno dei protagonisti della squadra biancoceleste.

La distanza economica tra le parti è di circa 20 milioni di euro: l’offerta della Juventus si aggirerebbe sui 40 milioni, mentre Lotito non vorrebbe scendere sotto i 60. L’interesse dei bianconeri per Taylor è concreto, ma l’intenzione di intervenire sul centrocampo da gennaio non coincide necessariamente con i tempi di un’eventuale operazione per il giocatore.

L’affare potrebbe infatti andare in porto in vista della prossima stagione. Molto dipenderà dalle competizioni che Lazio e Juventus disputeranno allora. La possibilità di un trasferimento resta quindi legata sia alla distanza tra la cifra ipotizzata per l’offerta e la richiesta di Lotito, sia agli impegni che attendono le due squadre nella stagione successiva.

#2475. European Roundup: Woltemade & Baur deliver strong performances after Klopp snub, Rose's Bournemouth record seventh straight win, Hürzeler's Brighton yet to concede

German coaches Marco Rose and Fabian Hürzeler succeeded once again with their English clubs, while Nick Woltemade and Mika Baur delivered strong performances after missing out from Germany's squad.

It wasn't all smooth sailing for Rose at the start of his Bournemouth reign, but the Cherries have extended their winning streak to seven games with a 3-1 win at Sparta Prague on Thursday evening. The visitors missed a first-half penalty-kick before securing a comeback win against their ten-man hosts.

USMNT international Tyler Adams scored in the European stage for the first time since his goal that helped RB Leipzig beat Atlético Madrid in the 2019/20 Champions League quarter-final round. Bournemouth are one of the three teams with a perfect Europa League record after four matchdays, alongside Benfica and Ferencváros, who stunned AC Milan with a 2-0 win at San Siro.

From 108 participating clubs, fellow Premier League side Brighton are the only team yet to concede a goal in UEFA competitions as they beat Getafe in Spain with Malick Yalcouye's solitary strike. Hürzeler's men sit in fifth place in the early standings, with Lugano, Panathinaikos, Twente and Monaco winning all their respective first three games in Conference League.

Germany U21 international Paris Brunner had his goal ruled out by VAR for an offside in Monaco's 2-0 win over FC Nordsjælland. The 20-year-old was a candidate for a first Germany call-up, but the name of the former BVB youngster wasn't found when Jürgen Klopp announced the squad earlier on Thursday.

Similarly, Baur and Woltemade helped their respective clubs to massive European wins after they failed to keep their spots in Germany's setup. Baur’s Celtic recorded their first points in Europa League with an entertaining 3-2 win over Beşiktaş.

Woltemade once again started for Juventus as the Serie A outfit returned home with three points from AZ Alkmaar. The 24-year-old registered his fifth direct goal involvement of the competition, with an assist to former Eintracht Frankfurt striker Randal Kolo Muani in a 2-1 win for the visitors.

Marten Winkler is another German striker who has been performing well in Europe as he scored in back-to-back Europa League games for Anderlecht. Like the former Hertha BSC player, FC Midtjylland's Kjell Wätjen was a 2. Bundesliga midfielder last term with VfL Bochum.

However, the ex-Dortmund youth midfielder had a difficult night on Thursday as his first-half red card led to his club's 6-1 home defeat to Ajax. The historic Dutch side missed Julian Brandt due to an injury, but Germany's current first-choice goalkeeper Marc-André ter Stegen and Thilo Kehrer played the entire game.

The Netherlands came back from 2-0 down to earn a 2-2 draw against Greece in the Nations League. Here is our player ratings for the Netherlands players.

Bart Verbruggen 5.5/10: Wasn’t happy with his defence in the first half. Could have done more for the first goal but did make a good save. Had nothing to do after the break.

Denzel Dumfries 5/10: Struggled in the first half before improving in the second. Had two goals disallowed for offside.

Micky van de Ven 5/10: His pace makes up for the gaps he leaves at times and in the firs half he struggled. Like others he got better in the second but had less to do.

Jurrien Timber 4/10: Looked very much like a player who is not fully fit. Second to everything and gave away fouls in the first half. Was too slow too react for the second goal.

Virgil van Dijk 6/10: Defensively he struggled in the first but his goal got his side back into the game after the break.

Joey Veerman 6.5/10: Always looking to make something happen going forward. Not everything worked but was always available. His corner led to a goal an he almost scored a free kick.

Quinten Timber 4/10: Disastrous display as he gave away foul after foul in the first half. Was rightly hooked at half time.

Guus Til 4/10: Like Timber, he offered nothing in the first half and was rightly taken off. Xavi wanted to give him a chance but he didn’t take it.

Crysencio Summerville 6/10: Was Netherlands only outlet going forward in the first half and is a constant threat on the right hand side.

Ruben van Bommel 4.5/10: Was too static at times and his error led to the second goal. Taken off at the break.

Mexx Meerdink 5/10: Tried but was isolated at times. Had no service in the first half and barely touched the ball in the second.

Noa Lang 7/10: Offered more on the left than Van Bommel as he took the game to the Greek defence. His cross led to the equaliser. Likely to start against Serbia after this display.

Tijjani Reijnders 6.5/10: Came on and scored the equaliser with a good finish. Did a lot more running than Til did and it was much needed energy.

Ryan Gravenberch 6.5/10: Immediately helped the Netherlands midfield gain control back in the second half.

Joshua Zirkzee N/A: Not on the pitch long enough for a review

Gjivai Zechiel N/A: Not on the pitch long enough for a review

#2470. Inter-Como, garanzia di spettacolo: 71 gol in due, i precedenti parlano chiaro

Inter-Como, garanzia di spettacolo: 71 gol in due, i precedenti parlano chiaro

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli FcInter1908 come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

L'ultima giornata di Serie A prima della nuova sosta per le Nazionali proporrà la sfida tra Inter e Como. Due squadre reduci dalle vittorie nei rispettivi impegni di Champions League, un match che promette gol e spettacolo. Così scrive il Corriere dello Sport: "Spettacolo a San Siro. Difficile che non accada con Inter e Como che giusto domenica pomeriggio si troveranno di fronte, dopo la scorpacciata di gol nelle rispettive gare di Champions".

"Intendiamoci, tra le squadre italiane, nerazzurri e lariani non sono il top, con i rispettivi bottini di 34 e 37 reti. La Roma, infatti, la spunta comunque di una lunghezza, con 38. Tuttavia, i precedenti tra le squadre di Chivu e Fabregas sono una garanzia. Nei 6 incroci delle ultime due stagioni, infatti, il "raccolto" complessivo è stato di 20 gol, vale a dire 3,33 a partita. Ma il meglio si è visto soprattutto nelle ultime due sfide. Quella di ritorno in campionato, è finita addirittura con un 4-3 per l'Inter. Il secondo match di semifinale di Coppa Italia invece con un 3-2, comunque per la formazione di Chivu. Con il tratto in comune che, in entrambe le occasioni, Nico Paz e compagni erano riusciti a scappare sul 2-0. Non è bastato, però, per strappare una vittoria. E, infatti, il bilancio è tutto dalla parte dei nerazzurri: 5 vittorie e un pareggio. E, quest'ultimo, è stato uno scialbo 0-0, nell'andata della semifinale di Coppa Italia: insomma, una sorta di eccezione per confermare la regola".

#2469. Zirkzee fires Netherlands to win over Serbia

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 11 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 10 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 9 of the Eredivisie ...

Football-Oranje's Michael and Abdul review round 8 of the Eredivisie ...
